There are two parts: a \"filter\" (which can filter out\n * input rows) and \"transforms\" (which can add fields to input rows). Filters may refer to fields generated by\n * a transform.\n *\n * See {@link Transform} for details on how each transform works.\n */\npublic class TransformSpec\n{\n  public static final TransformSpec NONE = new TransformSpec(null, null);\n\n  private final DimFilter filter;\n  private final List<Transform> transforms;\n\n  @JsonCreator\n  public TransformSpec(\n      @JsonProperty(\"filter\") final DimFilter filter,\n      @JsonProperty(\"transforms\") final List<Transform> transforms\n  )\n  {\n    this.filter = filter;\n    this.transforms = transforms == null ? ImmutableList.of() : transforms;\n\n    // Check for name collisions.\n    final Set<String> seen = new HashSet<>();\n    for (Transform transform : this.transforms) {\n      if (!seen.add(transform.getName())) {\n        throw new ISE(\"Transform name '%s' cannot be used twice\", transform.getName());\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  public static <T> TransformSpec fromInputRowParser(final InputRowParser<T> parser)\n  {\n    // Hack: some firehoses and input specs must extract transformSpec from the parser, since they do not\n    // actually use the parser, but still must respect the transformSpec. This method should extract whatever\n    // transformSpec \"decorate\" had put in.\n\n    if (parser instanceof TransformingInputRowParser) {\n      return ((TransformingInputRowParser) parser).getTransformSpec();\n    } else if (parser instanceof TransformingStringInputRowParser) {\n      return ((TransformingStringInputRowParser) parser).getTransformSpec();\n    } else {\n      throw new ISE(\"Parser was not decorated, but should have been\");\n    }\n  }\n\n  @JsonProperty\n  @Nullable\n  public DimFilter getFilter()\n  {\n    return filter;\n  }\n\n  @JsonProperty\n  public List<Transform> getTransforms()\n  {\n    return transforms;\n  }\n\n  public <T> InputRowParser<T> decorate(final InputRowParser<T> parser)\n  {\n    // Always decorates, even if the transformSpec is a no-op. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_41","type":"reasoning","text":"**Analyzing Jackson injection with empty key**\n\nI'm investigating how an empty JSON property key could bypass Jackson's injection protections, focusing on `GuiceAnnotationIntrospector` and `GuiceInjectableValues` which may override injectable value IDs ignoring the usual `@JacksonInject.value()`."}}
{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_42","type":"reasoning","text":"**Tracing injection ID resolution across introspectors**\n\nI confirmed Guice introspector ignores `JacksonInject.value()` using generic type Key as injection ID, while default Jackson introspector considers the annotation value. The combined introspector tries Guice first, then Jackson’s, but Guice returns Key if annotation is present, causing injection ID mismatch with JSON property names. This suggests the vulnerability may arise from how empty-string JSON properties interact with filter deserialization or `@JsonAnySetter` usage in filter base classes, prompting inspection of `DimFilter` and its hierarchy."}}

{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_57","type":"reasoning","text":"**Analyzing Jackson module setup and injectables**\n\nJacksonModule provides a default ObjectMapper without injectables, while DruidSecondaryModule sets up a @Json mapper with injectables via runtime setup. This suggests different ObjectMapper instances are used depending on context, impacting how JacksonInject works. The presence of FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES false means unknown JSON keys (including empty ones) are ignored but don't trigger errors, so the empty key exploit likely involves JavaScriptConfig being enabled by config rather than Jackson alone."}}
{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_58","type":"reasoning","text":"**Investigating empty key injection via JacksonInject handling**\n\nThe empty string key likely exploits how Jackson resolves `@JacksonInject` with a default empty value ID when the injectable is missing. If the ObjectMapper used (e.g., in Jersey) lacks the Guice injectable introspector or appropriate InjectableValues, Jackson treats the empty string key as a legitimate JSON property, allowing override of JavaScriptConfig. This suggests the fix should ensure the Jersey ObjectMapper uses GuiceInjectableValues or disallow empty property names to block the bypass."}}
